1→"""Tests for cluster completion guard in plan done."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.app.commands.plan.override_handlers import (
     6→    _check_cluster_guard,
     7→    _CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD,
     8→)
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan, ensure_plan_defaults
    10→
    11→
    12→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    13→# Helpers
    14→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15→
    16→def _plan_with_cluster(name: str, finding_ids: list[str]) -> dict:
    17→    plan = empty_plan()
    18→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    19→    plan["clusters"][name] = {
    20→        "name": name,
    21→        "finding_ids": finding_ids,
    22→        "auto": True,
    23→        "cluster_key": f"auto::{name}",
    24→        "action": "autofix",
    25→        "user_modified": False,
    26→    }
    27→    return plan
    28→
    29→
    30→def _state_with_findings(*ids: str) -> dict:
    31→    findings = {}
    32→    for fid in ids:
    33→        findings[fid] = {
    34→            "id": fid,
    35→            "status": "open",
    36→            "detector": "test",
    37→            "file": "test.py",
    38→            "tier": 1,
    39→            "confidence": "high",
    40→            "summary": f"Finding {fid}",
    41→        }
    42→    return {"findings": findings}
    43→
    44→
    45→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    46→# Tests
    47→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    48→
    49→def test_cluster_guard_blocks_small_cluster():
    50→    """Clusters with <= threshold items should be blocked."""
    51→    ids = [f"f{i}" for i in range(5)]
    52→    plan = _plan_with_cluster("auto/test", ids)
    53→    state = _state_with_findings(*ids)
    54→
    55→    blocked = _check_cluster_guard(["auto/test"], plan, state)
    56→    assert blocked is True
    57→
    58→
    59→def test_cluster_guard_blocks_empty_cluster(capsys):
    60→    """Empty clusters should be blocked — must add items first."""
    61→    plan = _plan_with_cluster("auto/test", [])
    62→    state = _state_with_findings()
    63→
    64→    blocked = _check_cluster_guard(["auto/test"], plan, state)
    65→    assert blocked is True
    66→
    67→    captured = capsys.readouterr()
    68→    assert "empty" in captured.out.lower()
    69→    assert "add items" in captured.out.lower()
    70→
    71→
    72→def test_cluster_guard_allows_large_cluster():
    73→    """Clusters with > threshold items should be allowed."""
    74→    ids = [f"f{i}" for i in range(_CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD + 1)]
    75→    plan = _plan_with_cluster("auto/test", ids)
    76→    state = _state_with_findings(*ids)
    77→
    78→    blocked = _check_cluster_guard(["auto/test"], plan, state)
    79→    assert blocked is False
    80→
    81→
    82→def test_cluster_guard_allows_non_cluster_pattern():
    83→    """Non-cluster patterns should not be blocked."""
    84→    plan = _plan_with_cluster("auto/test", ["f1", "f2"])
    85→    state = _state_with_findings("f1", "f2")
    86→
    87→    blocked = _check_cluster_guard(["f1"], plan, state)
    88→    assert blocked is False
    89→
    90→
    91→def test_cluster_guard_at_threshold_boundary():
    92→    """Exactly threshold items should be blocked."""
    93→    ids = [f"f{i}" for i in range(_CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD)]
    94→    plan = _plan_with_cluster("auto/test", ids)
    95→    state = _state_with_findings(*ids)
    96→
    97→    blocked = _check_cluster_guard(["auto/test"], plan, state)
    98→    assert blocked is True
    99→
   100→
   101→def test_cluster_guard_prints_items(capsys):
   102→    """Guard should print the items in the cluster."""
   103→    plan = _plan_with_cluster("auto/test", ["f1", "f2"])
   104→    state = _state_with_findings("f1", "f2")

1→"""Tests for queue order guard in resolve command."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import importlib
     6→from unittest.mock import MagicMock
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan, ensure_plan_defaults
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.persistence import save_plan
    10→
    11→
    12→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    13→# Lazy import helper — avoids the circular import in resolve/render
    14→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15→
    16→def _import_guard():
    17→    """Import _check_queue_order_guard, patching away the render dependency."""
    18→    import desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd as cmd_mod
    19→    return cmd_mod._check_queue_order_guard
    20→
    21→
    22→def _get_guard_fn():
    23→    """Return the guard function, handling circular import gracefully."""
    24→    try:
    25→        return _import_guard()
    26→    except ImportError:
    27→        # Fall back: patch the problematic render import chain
    28→        import sys
    29→        stub = MagicMock()
    30→        modules_to_stub = [
    31→            "desloppify.app.commands.helpers.score_update",
    32→            "desloppify.app.commands.scan.scan_helpers",
    33→        ]
    34→        saved = {}
    35→        for mod_name in modules_to_stub:
    36→            if mod_name in sys.modules:
    37→                saved[mod_name] = sys.modules[mod_name]
    38→            sys.modules[mod_name] = stub
    39→
    40→        # Force reimport
    41→        import desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd as cmd_mod
    42→        importlib.reload(cmd_mod)
    43→        fn = cmd_mod._check_queue_order_guard
    44→
    45→        # Restore
    46→        for mod_name in modules_to_stub:
    47→            if mod_name in saved:
    48→                sys.modules[mod_name] = saved[mod_name]
    49→            else:
    50→                sys.modules.pop(mod_name, None)
    51→        return fn
    52→
    53→
    54→_check_queue_order_guard = _get_guard_fn()
    55→
    56→
    57→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    58→# Helpers
    59→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    60→
    61→def _state_with_findings(*ids: str) -> dict:
    62→    findings = {}
    63→    for fid in ids:
    64→        findings[fid] = {
    65→            "id": fid,
    66→            "status": "open",
    67→            "detector": "unused",
    68→            "file": "test.py",
    69→            "tier": 1,
    70→            "confidence": "high",
    71→            "summary": f"Finding {fid}",
    72→        }
    73→    return {"findings": findings, "scan_count": 5}
    74→
    75→
    76→def _setup_plan(tmp_path, monkeypatch, queue_order: list[str], clusters: dict | None = None):
    77→    """Create and save a plan with given queue order, monkeypatch PLAN_FILE."""
    78→    import desloppify.engine._plan.persistence as persist_mod
    79→
    80→    plan_file = tmp_path / "plan.json"
    81→    plan = empty_plan()
    82→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    83→    plan["queue_order"] = queue_order
    84→    if clusters:
    85→        plan["clusters"] = clusters
    86→    save_plan(plan, plan_file)
    87→    monkeypatch.setattr(persist_mod, "PLAN_FILE", plan_file)
    88→
    89→
    90→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    91→# Tests
    92→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    93→
    94→def test_guard_blocks_out_of_order(tmp_path, monkeypatch, capsys):
    95→    """Resolving item #2 when item #1 is next should be blocked."""
    96→    state = _state_with_findings("a", "b", "c")
    97→    _setup_plan(tmp_path, monkeypatch, ["a", "b", "c"])
    98→
    99→    blocked = _check_queue_order_guard(state, ["b"], "fixed")
   100→    assert blocked is True
   101→
   102→    captured = capsys.readouterr()
   103→    assert "b" in captured.out
   104→    assert "plan order" in captured.out.lower() or "Queue order" in captured.out

   111→
   112→    blocked = _check_queue_order_guard(state, ["a"], "fixed")
   113→    assert blocked is False
   114→
   115→
   116→def test_guard_allows_non_fixed_status(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
   117→    """Non-fixed statuses (wontfix, open) bypass the guard."""
   118→    state = _state_with_findings("a", "b")
   119→    _setup_plan(tmp_path, monkeypatch, ["a", "b"])
   120→
   121→    blocked = _check_queue_order_guard(state, ["b"], "wontfix")

   152→def test_guard_blocks_item_behind_cluster(tmp_path, monkeypatch, capsys):

   176→def test_guard_no_plan(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
   177→    """No living plan → no blocking."""

   209→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

   212→
   213→def test_guard_skips_stale_ids_in_queue_order(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
   214→    """Stale IDs at the front of queue_order should not block live items."""
   215→    # "stale" is in queue_order but NOT in state findings at all
   216→    state = _state_with_findings("b", "c")
   217→    _setup_plan(tmp_path, monkeypatch, ["stale", "b", "c"])
   218→
   219→    # "b" should be treated as the effective front since "stale" is gone
   220→    blocked = _check_queue_order_guard(state, ["b"], "fixed")
   221→    assert blocked is False
   222→
   223→
   224→def test_guard_skips_resolved_ids_in_queue_order(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
   225→    """Resolved (non-open) IDs at the front of queue_order should not block."""
   226→    state = _state_with_findings("a", "b")
   227→    # Mark "a" as fixed — it's still in state but no longer open
   228→    state["findings"]["a"]["status"] = "fixed"
   229→    _setup_plan(tmp_path, monkeypatch, ["a", "b"])
   230→
   231→    # "b" should be next since "a" is resolved
   232→    blocked = _check_queue_order_guard(state, ["b"], "fixed")
   233→    assert blocked is False
   234→
   235→
   236→def test_guard_cluster_with_stale_members(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
   237→    """Cluster with stale members should not cause false queue-order violation."""
   238→    # "a" is alive, "stale_member" is not in state
   239→    state = _state_with_findings("a", "c")
   240→    _setup_plan(
   241→        tmp_path, monkeypatch,
   242→        ["a", "stale_member", "c"],
   243→        clusters={
   244→            "auto/unused": {
   245→                "name": "auto/unused",
   246→                "auto": True,
   247→                "cluster_key": "auto::unused",
   248→                "finding_ids": ["a", "stale_member"],
   249→                "description": "Fix 2 unused",
   250→                "action": "desloppify autofix unused --dry-run",
   251→                "user_modified": False,
   252→            },
   253→        },
   254→    )
   255→
   256→    # Resolving the cluster should not be blocked by its stale member
   257→    blocked = _check_queue_order_guard(state, ["auto/unused"], "fixed")
   258→    assert blocked is False
   259→
   260→
   261→def test_guard_all_resolved_ids_stale(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
   262→    """If all resolved IDs are stale, guard should not block."""
   263→    state = _state_with_findings("a")
   264→    _setup_plan(tmp_path, monkeypatch, ["stale_1", "stale_2", "a"])
   265→
   266→    # Trying to resolve only stale IDs → nothing to block
   267→    blocked = _check_queue_order_guard(state, ["stale_1"], "fixed")
   268→    assert blocked is False
   269→

1→"""Shared plan constants and helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→CONFIDENCE_ORDER = {"high": 0, "medium": 1, "low": 2}
     6→
     7→
     8→def is_subjective_phase(phase) -> bool:
     9→    """Return True when a phase is subjective/review oriented."""
    10→    label = (phase.label or "").lower()
    11→    run_name = getattr(phase.run, "__name__", "").lower()
    12→    return (
    13→        "subjective" in label
    14→        or "review" in label
    15→        or run_name == "phase_subjective_review"
    16→    )
    17→

## 1. MAIN SCAN WORKFLOW & FINDING COLLECTION

### Entry Point: `run_scan_generation()`
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py`  
**Lines:** 569-604

```python
def run_scan_generation(
    runtime: ScanRuntime,
) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], dict[str, object], dict[str, object] | None]:
    """Run detector pipeline and return findings, potentials, and codebase metrics."""
    enable_file_cache()
    enable_parse_cache()
    try:
        findings, potentials = plan_mod.generate_findings(  # Line 576
            runtime.path,
            lang=runtime.lang,
            options=PlanScanOptions(
                include_slow=runtime.effective_include_slow,
                zone_overrides=runtime.zone_overrides,
                profile=runtime.profile,
            ),
        )
    finally:
        disable_parse_cache()
        disable_file_cache()
    # ... augmentation steps (stale exclusions, wontfix) ...
    return findings, potentials, codebase_metrics
```

### Phase Execution Pipeline: `generate_findings()`
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/scan.py`  
**Lines:** 134-151

```python
def generate_findings(
    path: Path,
    lang: LangConfig | LangRun | None = None,
    *,
    options: PlanScanOptions | None = None,
) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
    """Run all detectors and convert results to normalized findings."""
    resolved_options = options or PlanScanOptions()
    resolved_lang = _resolve_lang(lang, PROJECT_ROOT)
    runtime_lang = make_lang_run(resolved_lang)
    return _generate_findings_from_lang(
        path,
        runtime_lang,
        include_slow=resolved_options.include_slow,
        zone_overrides=resolved_options.zone_overrides,
        profile=resolved_options.profile,
    )
```

### Phase Selection & Execution: `_run_phases()`
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/planning/scan.py`  
**Lines:** 73-94

```python
def _select_phases(lang: LangRun, *, include_slow: bool, profile: str) -> list[DetectorPhase]:
    """Select phases based on profile and inclusion flags."""
    active_profile = profile if profile in {"objective", "full", "ci"} else "full"
    phases = lang.phases
    if not include_slow or active_profile == "ci":
        phases = [phase for phase in phases if not phase.slow]
    if active_profile in {"objective", "ci"}:
        phases = [phase for phase in phases if not is_subjective_phase(phase)]
    return phases

def _run_phases(path: Path, lang: LangRun, phases: list[DetectorPhase]) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
    """Execute phases and collect findings."""
    findings: list[Finding] = []
    all_potentials: dict[str, int] = {}

    total = len(phases)
    for idx, phase in enumerate(phases, start=1):
        _stderr(f"  [{idx}/{total}] {phase.label}...")
        phase_findings, phase_potentials = phase.run(path, lang)  # Line 90: CALLS PHASE
        all_potentials.update(phase_potentials)
        findings.extend(phase_findings)  # Line 92: ADDS TO FINDINGS LIST
    
    return findings, all_potentials
```

### Key Points:
- **Line 90:** `phase.run(path, lang)` — each `DetectorPhase` is called with the path and language runtime
- **Line 92:** `findings.extend(phase_findings)` — phase results are appended to a single growing list
- **Return type:** List of dictionaries (Finding type), plus potentials dict
- **NO DEDUPLICATION** happens at phase collection time — findings are simply accumulated

### Stamps Finding Context: `_stamp_finding_context()`
**Lines:** 97-114  
Adds `lang` and `zone` fields to each finding before returning.

## 2. FINDING TYPE & ID STRUCTURE

### Finding TypedDict Definition
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/schema.py`  
**Lines:** 51-72

```python
class Finding(TypedDict):
    """The central data structure: a normalized finding from any detector."""

    id: str                # FINDING ID (structured: detector::file::name)
    detector: str          # Detector name (e.g., "unused", "complexity")
    file: str              # Relative file path
    tier: int              # Severity tier (1-5)
    confidence: str        # "high", "medium", "low"
    summary: str           # Human-readable finding title
    detail: dict[str, Any] # Detector-specific metadata
    status: FindingStatus  # "open", "fixed", "auto_resolved", "wontfix", "false_positive"
    note: str | None       # User-added notes
    first_seen: str        # ISO timestamp
    last_seen: str         # ISO timestamp
    resolved_at: str | None# ISO timestamp when status changed
    reopen_count: int      # Number of times reopened
    suppressed: NotRequired[bool]
    suppressed_at: NotRequired[str | None]
    suppression_pattern: NotRequired[str | None]
    resolution_attestation: NotRequired[dict[str, str | bool | None]]
    lang: NotRequired[str] # Language name (added at scan time)
    zone: NotRequired[str] # Zone classification (added at scan time)
```

### Finding ID Construction: `make_finding()`
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py`  
**Lines:** 147-175

```python
def make_finding(
    detector: str,
    file: str,
    name: str,
    *,
    tier: int,
    confidence: str,
    summary: str,
    detail: dict | None = None,
) -> Finding:
    """Create a normalized finding dict with a stable ID."""
    rfile = rel(file)
    finding_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
    now = utc_now()
    return {
        "id": finding_id,
        "detector": detector,
        "file": rfile,
        "tier": tier,
        "confidence": confidence,
        "summary": summary,
        "detail": detail or {},
        "status": "open",
        "note": None,
        "first_seen": now,
        "last_seen": now,
        "resolved_at": None,
        "reopen_count": 0,
    }
```

**Finding ID Structure (Line 159):**
- Format: `detector::relative_file_path::entity_name` (if name provided)
- Or: `detector::relative_file_path` (if name is empty/None)
- Example: `complexity::src/utils.py::calculate_foo` or `unused::tests/test_api.py`

## 3. FINDINGS MERGE & DEDUPLICATION

### Main Merge Entry: `merge_scan()`
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/merge.py`  
**Lines:** 108-217

```python
def merge_scan(
    state: StateModel,
    current_findings: list[dict],  # Findings from _run_phases
    options: MergeScanOptions | None = None,
) -> ScanDiff:
    """Merge a fresh scan into existing state and return a diff summary."""
    ensure_state_defaults(state)
    # ... metadata recording ...
    
    existing = state["findings"]
    ignore_patterns = (
        resolved_options.ignore
        if resolved_options.ignore is not None
        else state.get("config", {}).get("ignore", [])
    )
    current_ids, new_count, reopened_count, current_by_detector, ignored_count, upsert_changed = (
        upsert_findings(  # Line 140: DEDUP HAPPENS HERE
            existing,
            current_findings,
            ignore_patterns,
            now,
            lang=resolved_options.lang,
        )
    )
    # ... rest of merge logic ...
```

### Actual Deduplication: `upsert_findings()`
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/merge_findings.py`  
**Lines:** 117-204

```python
def upsert_findings(
    existing: dict,                    # Previous findings from state
    current_findings: list[dict],      # Findings from this scan
    ignore: list[str],
    now: str,
    *,
    lang: str | None,
) -> tuple[set[str], int, int, dict[str, int], int, set[str]]:
    """Insert new findings and update existing ones.
    
    Returns (current_ids, new_count, reopened_count, by_detector, ignored_count, changed_detectors).
    """
    current_ids: set[str] = set()  # Line 129: DEDUP TRACKER
    new_count = reopened_count = ignored_count = 0
    by_detector: dict[str, int] = {}
    changed_detectors: set[str] = set()

    for finding in current_findings:
        finding_id = finding["id"]
        detector = finding.get("detector", "unknown")
        current_ids.add(finding_id)  # Line 137: TRACK THIS ID
        by_detector[detector] = by_detector.get(detector, 0) + 1
        matched_ignore = matched_ignore_pattern(finding_id, finding["file"], ignore)
        if matched_ignore:
            ignored_count += 1

        if lang:
            finding["lang"] = lang

        if finding_id not in existing:  # Line 146: NEW FINDING
            existing[finding_id] = dict(finding)
            if matched_ignore:
                existing[finding_id]["suppressed"] = True
                existing[finding_id]["suppressed_at"] = now
                existing[finding_id]["suppression_pattern"] = matched_ignore
                continue
            new_count += 1
            changed_detectors.add(detector)
            continue

        # Line 157: EXISTING FINDING — UPDATE IT
        previous = existing[finding_id]
        previous.update(
            last_seen=now,
            tier=finding["tier"],
            confidence=finding["confidence"],
            summary=finding["summary"],
            detail=finding.get("detail", {}),
        )
        # ... suppress/unsuppress logic ...
        if previous["status"] in ("fixed", "auto_resolved"):  # Line 180: REOPEN IF NEEDED
            # ... reopen logic ...
            reopened_count += 1
            changed_detectors.add(detector)

    return current_ids, new_count, reopened_count, by_detector, ignored_count, changed_detectors
```

**Key Deduplication Logic:**
- **Line 129:** `current_ids: set[str] = set()` — tracking set for ID uniqueness
- **Line 137:** Each finding ID from `current_findings` is added to the set
- **Line 146:** `if finding_id not in existing:` — checks if it's a new finding
  - If new: adds to state
  - If exists: updates `last_seen`, `tier`, `confidence`, `summary`, `detail` fields
- **NO DUPLICATE DETECTION IN CURRENT_FINDINGS** — if the same ID appears twice in `current_findings`, the second occurrence silently overwrites the first (dictionary key collision)

## 5. CRITICAL FINDING: NO DEDUP IN PHASE COLLECTION

**The vulnerability you're looking for:**

In `_run_phases()` at **line 92** of `scan.py`:
```python
findings.extend(phase_findings)  # Naive append — NO dedup
```

If two phases return findings with the **same ID**, they both end up in the list:
```python
[
    {"id": "complexity::file.py::foo", ...from phase_1},
    {"id": "complexity::file.py::foo", ...from phase_2},  # DUPLICATE!
]
```

When `upsert_findings()` processes this list (line 134-203 of `merge_findings.py`), it iterates once per item. The second occurrence **overwrites** the first in the `existing` dict (because dict keys are unique), but both are counted in `new_count` or statistics if they're both new.

---

## 6. WHERE TO ADD THE GUARD

**Best location:** Inside `_run_phases()` after line 92, before returning findings.

```python
def _run_phases(path: Path, lang: LangRun, phases: list[DetectorPhase]) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
    findings: list[Finding] = []
    all_potentials: dict[str, int] = {}

    for idx, phase in enumerate(phases, start=1):
        phase_findings, phase_potentials = phase.run(path, lang)
        all_potentials.update(phase_potentials)
        findings.extend(phase_findings)
    
    # ADD DEDUP GUARD HERE (before _stamp_finding_context)
    _check_findings_dedup_guard(findings)  # NEW GUARD
    
    return findings, all_potentials
```

Or call it in `_generate_findings_from_lang()` after `_run_phases()` completes.
